# Calibration Log — scoring the forecasts

The scoreboard. Every forecast that enters `assumptions.json` gets scored here when
reality lands. Newest first.

**Why this file is the most important one in the repo:** portfolio return measures
the market as much as the manager. Calibration measures only the manager. A book can
be up 30% in a year where every thesis was wrong, and down 10% in a year where every
call was right. Only this file can tell the difference — and only this file can turn
"I have a process" into "I have an edge," because an edge is a claim about accuracy
and accuracy is a measurable thing.

---

## How to score

When an actual lands, add a row. Score three separate things — they come apart more
often than people expect:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Forecast** | What was written, with its date and probability |
| **Actual** | What happened, with the source |
| **Direction** | ✅ / ❌ — was the base case on the right side? |
| **Error** | Actual vs the probability-weighted estimate, in % |
| **Reason** | ✅ right for the stated reason · ⚠️ right for a *different* reason · ❌ wrong |
| **Lesson** | One line. What changes in the model, or nothing |

The **Reason** column is the one that compounds. Right-for-the-wrong-reason is a
process failure that happens to pay, and it must be recorded as a failure or the
process learns the wrong lesson and repeats it with more confidence.

### Aggregate scoring (recompute quarterly, once n ≥ 10)

- **Hit rate by confidence bucket** — of forecasts marked 0.8 confidence, how many landed?
  Well-calibrated means ~80%. Systematically lower means overconfidence, and the
  `confidence` field in `assumptions.json` should be cut across the board.
- **Bias** — mean signed error. Persistently positive means the base cases are
  optimistic, which is the single most common flaw in a private book.
- **Dispersion check** — how often does the actual land *outside* the bear–bull range?
  Should be rare (<15%). More than that and the scenarios are too narrow, which
  understates risk everywhere downstream, including in the optimizer's vol estimates.

### Annotations on open forecasts

**#10 · Rebar — the final ISSUED 29-Jul-2026. Not scored: the rates are not in hand. Read
pre-registered 2026-07-30 03:53.**
Commerce issued the final determinations for Vietnam, Egypt and Bulgaria on 29-Jul. The rates are not
yet obtainable — `federalregister.gov` 403s from here and Vietnamese press still quotes preliminaries.

**The read, registered before the numbers arrive.** Provisional duties near 122% have been collected
since March, under the extension HPG itself requested. **HPG has therefore already traded a full
quarter under them, and that quarter printed revenue +53% and NPAT +51%.** The commercial effect of
losing US rebar is already inside the reported run-rate rather than ahead of it. If the forecast
scores as a HIT, *this* is the reason it should be credited to — not "the rate came in low."

**And the scoring trap is explicit:** four numbers circulate for this one case — petition 117.61%,
HPG preliminary 121.97%, all-others preliminary 130.77%, and the unpublished final. **Only Hoa Phat's
final rate scores this forecast.** Press already headlines "130%" alongside Hoa Phat's name; that is
the all-others rate and must not be used.

**#10 · Rebar — the forecast measures two duties, not one. Clarified 2026-07-29 20:54.**
The forecast reads "US rebar AD/CVD final ≈ headline risk only, <3% of revenue, no thesis change".
Scoring it requires reading **both** rates, and they are very different animals: the **antidumping**
preliminary is **121.97%**, the **countervailing** preliminary is **1.08%** ad valorem. The two
finals are aligned to issue on the same date. So the CVD side is already effectively resolved in the
forecast's favour, and essentially the whole test rests on the AD final.

The date itself also came from Hoa Phat: it requested the postponement on 03-Mar-2026, moving the
final from "no later than 26-May" into late July. **The 28-Jul date elapsed with nothing published**,
so this forecast is not late in resolving — it has no published resolution to score against yet.

**#4 · MBB — a pre-print read registered 2026-07-29 19:53, before the statement.**
Forecast #4 resolves tomorrow. The on-file Q2 PBT estimate is ₫8,812bn, **+18% YoY**. Peers that
have now actually printed are running the other way: **ACB −12.06%** and **VIB −8.24%**, with
Saigonbank at a loss. The mechanism in ACB's numbers is specific and testable — **net interest
income +16.5%, profit −12%, the whole gap being provisions.** Sector provisioning is forecast +19%.

**~~Strengthened 2026-07-30 09:53~~ — WITHDRAWN 2026-07-30 20:54, still before the print.** I
strengthened this read from "peers suggest" to "MBB was already doing this", on the basis of press
describing MBB's Q1 as having *"a sharp increase in risk provisioning"*. **I now have the number and
it does not support that.** MBB's Q1 provisioning was **₫3,455bn, +15.7%** — against Q1 PBT of
**+14.8%**. Provisioning grew **in line with the business**, not disproportionately.

**That is not the ACB pattern.** ACB's Q2 had net interest income **+16.5%** and profit **−12%** —
provisions overwhelming a growing top line. MBB's Q1 shows nothing of the kind.

**The original read stands on its own footing**; it rests on ACB and VIB actually printing −12% and
−8%, which is unchanged. What does not stand is the claim that MBB itself was already showing the
pattern. **Withdrawn before the print, because withdrawing it afterwards would be indistinguishable
from retrofitting.**

**So the registered read is: if MBB misses, expect it to miss on credit cost with net interest
income intact, not on revenue.** Recording it now rather than after the print is the whole point —
a mechanism identified afterwards is a story, identified beforehand it is a test. If MBB instead
misses on the top line, or beats outright, this read is wrong and gets scored as wrong.



Original rows are **never edited** — a forecast that gets quietly revised as evidence
arrives cannot be scored, and a calibration log that permits revision measures nothing.
Where the evidence moves before resolution, it is recorded here instead, and both the
original and the annotation are visible when the row is finally scored.

- **2026-07-27 · #9 (VCI, 9M PBT ≥ 55% of the FY target, conf 0.65).** The 0.65 now looks
  clearly too high, and it looked too high on the day it was written. Clearing the
  threshold requires **₫1,265bn** of 9M pre-tax profit; the half delivered **₫667bn**; so
  **Q3 alone must produce ₫598bn — 2.21× the ₫270.8bn Q2 actual and 1.51× the ₫396bn
  Q1**. Only the signed IB/IPO pipeline landing inside Q3 gets there. Two facts arrived
  today that make the miss more likely rather than less: peers are not short (SSI finished
  the half at **53%** of its full-year target against VCI's 29%), and VCI's margin book was
  **flat quarter-on-quarter** while the system's grew 7% to a record.
  **The lesson is already visible and does not need the outcome to land:** a confidence of
  0.65 was attached to a forecast whose required arithmetic was never computed. Computing
  it took two minutes. **Confidence should not be set on a plan-completion forecast until
  the implied per-quarter run-rate has been worked out** — that check now belongs in the
  screen, not in the post-mortem.

**Forecast #2 now has an exact resolution test (added 2026-07-28).** It predicts HPG's Q2
core NPAT per tonne comes in **below ₫1.60m**. Until now the denominator was an estimate,
so the print could have been argued either way. HPG has since disclosed Q2 sales of **3.5m
tonnes**, which fixes it. The forecast therefore resolves cleanly at a headline core NPAT
of **₫5,600bn**: below that and the forecast is right, above it and it is wrong. Street
sits at ₫6,400–6,500bn, implying ₫1.83–1.86m/t. **Scoring this one must also apply the
Reason column strictly** — the pre-registered note in `hpg-spread-bridge.md` §7 says a Q2
beat is what the inventory lag predicts and is not evidence against the thesis. If the
forecast misses, it misses; that argument explains the timing, it does not rescue the call.

## Scored forecasts

### #2 · HPG Q2 core NPAT per tonne — **MISS**, resolved 2026-07-29

| Field | |
|---|---|
| **Forecast** | 26-Jul: Q2 core NPAT/tonne **below ₫1.60m**, as the Formosa/iron-ore spread squeeze bites. Bear branch p=0.45. Threshold fixed 28-Jul at ₫5,600bn core on the disclosed 3.5m-tonne basket. |
| **Actual** | Q2 NPAT **₫6,424bn** (+51% YoY), on 3.5m tonnes = **₫1.835m/t**. T1, filed 29-Jul. |
| **Direction** | ❌ Wrong side. Predicted below ₫1.60m; landed just under the **bull** branch of ₫1.85m. |
| **Error** | **+14.7%** above the ₫5,600bn threshold. Against the probability-weighted branch mean the miss is larger still, since bear carried p=0.45. |
| **Reason** | ❌ **Wrong, and wrong in a way that was visible before the print.** |

**The forecast contradicted my own analysis, and both were on file.** The `TIMING` note in
`hpg-spread-bridge.md` says plainly: ore and coal are bought one to two quarters forward, so Q2
burns cheap Q1 inputs and *"a strong Q2 print does NOT refute the bear thesis"* — the squeeze
lands in Q3. That note was written the same day as the forecast. **One said Q2 would be weak; the
other said Q2 could not yet be weak.** I never reconciled them.

The 28 July consistency audit checked *fields against fields* and found four errors. It never
checked a **forecast against a note**, which is where this one was hiding in plain sight.

**Lesson.** A forecast must be checked against every standing note on the same variable before it
is logged, not only against the data. Where a note explains why a period is uninformative, no
forecast should be registered on that period at all — the honest version of #2 would have been a
**Q3** forecast.

**What survives the miss.** The spread bridge's spot-persists arithmetic is untouched: at current
input prices it computes ₫0.60–0.68m/t, and the newly filed H1 makes the bear branch require
**exactly** that (H2 of ₫6,704bn over 7–9m tonnes = ₫0.745–0.958m/t). The thesis was mistimed, not
disproved. It now resolves in Q3, and that is where the next forecast belongs.


**Read that as a warning, not a placeholder.** Ten open forecasts and zero scored
means every confidence weight now driving the optimizer is an assertion, not a
measurement. The engine's arithmetic is sound; its inputs are so far unaudited by
reality. Treat the first scoring round as the moment this system starts being worth
something.

---

### #1 · KDH Q2/2026 — **MISS**, and by a factor of five on the part that was falsifiable

**Resolved 2026-07-30.** Q2 revenue **₫161bn (−85% YoY)**; H1 revenue ₫442bn (−75%); H1 net profit
**₫321bn (−6%)** against Q1 of ~₫327bn, so **Q2 profit was approximately zero**.

**Scored against the pre-registered rule, not the range.** That rule already declared the stated
range (₫170–348bn) close to unfalsifiable and said it must not score as a hit. It does not — but it
is worth recording that **even a range spanning the entire published consensus was missed, on the
low side.** Every house was too high; VCBS's ₫1,123bn revenue forecast was **seven times** the ₫161bn
outcome.

**The falsifiable part was the units read**, which mapped ₫170bn ⇒ ~19 units, ₫259bn ⇒ ~27,
₫348bn ⇒ ~35. **Actual: ₫161bn of revenue at ₫42–44bn ASP is ~3.7–3.8 units.** The mapping was
**five times too high at its most conservative point.**

**Right in direction, badly wrong in calibration — and that is the finding.** The dossier's
bull-infeasibility work was pointing the right way: it said handovers could not reach the bull case,
and handovers indeed collapsed. But *every* branch, including bear at 80 units for the year, is now
far above the run-rate — roughly **10 units handed in H1 against a bear branch needing 80**. Being
directionally right while the entire scenario tree sits above the outcome is not a success. **The
tree was calibrated off Q1's six units and a sold book, and never off the possibility that handovers
would simply stop.**

**What actually happened, per the filing:** financial costs ×3 and selling expenses ×2.9, on payment
discounts and customer-support costs. **The company was discounting hard and still not handing over
units.** H1 operating cash flow ≈ **−₫2,580bn**, borrowings **+₫6,500bn**, inventory **>₫23,000bn**.

**Process note recorded regardless of outcome, as the rule required:** writing down the consensus
span and calling it a forecast was a process failure. It has now also proved an expensive one — the
span was wide enough to feel safe and still missed.

### #10 · US rebar final — **HIT**, and right for the stated reason. First hit of three scored.

**Resolved 2026-07-30.** Federal Register **2026-15438** (AD) and **2026-15437** (CVD), both applicable
30-Jul. **Final dumping margins for Vietnam 128.53%–136.57%**, cash deposit rates **123.49%–131.53%**
after an export-subsidy offset. Preliminary was 121.97%–130.77%.

**The rate went the "wrong" way and the forecast still holds — which is the right outcome for the
right reason.** The forecast did not predict the rate; it predicted the *impact*: "headline risk only,
<3% of revenue, no thesis change." Duties near 122% have been collected since March under the
extension HPG itself requested, so **HPG already traded a full quarter under them — and that quarter
printed revenue +53% and NPAT +51%.** Moving a prohibitive duty from ~122% to ~129% changes nothing
commercially. The trade was already uneconomic; the earnings effect is already in the run-rate.

**Not over-credited.** Two caveats are recorded rather than glossed. First, **the finals came in ~6.6pp
higher at both ends**, so anyone reading the forecast as "the rate will be mild" would have been
wrong — it was the *impact* claim that carried it. Second, **which end applies to Hoa Phat is not
confirmed**: the preliminary structure put the mandatory respondent at the lower rate, so 128.53% is
the likely figure, but that is inference from structure, not a reading of the table.
`federalregister.gov` 403s from this environment, so the rates are T5 trade press on a T1 document.

### #4 · MBB Q2/2026 — **MISS**, on the high side, and the pre-registered read is **wrong**

**Resolved 2026-07-31**, one day past the Circular 96/2020 deadline. Q2 PBT **₫10,560bn, +40%**;
Q2 NPAT-to-parent **₫8,229.06bn, +40.01%**; H1 NPAT-to-parent **₫15,744.58bn, +26.51%**.

**Scored on the forecast's own measure.** The pre-registered band was ₫6,347–7,757bn, and the
₫7,052bn centre was derived as ₫8,812bn PBT × 0.80 — i.e. **consolidated NPAT**, not
parent-attributable. Like for like, actual consolidated NPAT is ₫10,560 × 0.80 = **₫8,448bn**:
**+19.8% above the forecast**, ₫691bn outside the band. On parent-attributable the error is +16.7%.
**Miss on either measure**, and the band was chosen at ±10% precisely so that a 20% error could not
be argued into a hit afterwards.

**The pre-registered read is scored WRONG, on its own terms.** It read: *"if MBB misses, expect it
to miss on credit cost with net interest income intact, not on revenue"* — with the explicit
stipulation *"if MBB instead misses on the top line, or beats outright, this read is wrong and gets
scored as wrong."* **MBB beat outright.** Net interest income rose 36.55% to ₫16,893.65bn and profit
rose faster still. The conditional never triggered, and the clause that pre-committed to calling that
a failure is the reason this is recorded as one rather than quietly retired as "not applicable."

**Where the read came from, and why it failed.** It was built on a peer read-across: ACB printed Q2
PBT **−12%** on sharply higher provisions with net interest income +16.5%, and VIB **−8%**. The
inference was a sector provisioning cycle. **MBB printed +40% in the same quarter.** Same sector,
same quarter, same rate environment, opposite outcome — a spread of roughly **52 percentage points**
between ACB and MBB. NPL did drift up, 1.42% → 1.45%, but coverage *rose* 92.24% → 93.63%, which is
the opposite of a bank being forced to provide.

**So the read-across is the finding, not the miss.** The miss itself is unremarkable — a broker was
20% low. What is worth keeping is that **"peers are showing X, therefore this bank will show X" was
tested prospectively and failed**, on three banks in one quarter, and that the 30-Jul withdrawal was
correct for the right reason: when MBB's *own* Q1 provisioning turned out to be +15.7% against PBT
+14.8%, the bank-specific evidence contradicted the peer inference, and the bank-specific evidence
was right.

**One thing this does not resolve.** H1 provisioning is still unknown — the figure circulating in
Q2-context coverage was the Q1 number reappearing (see `assumptions.json`
`_TWO_FIGURES_IN_THE_SAME_COVERAGE_WERE_STALE_2026_07_31`). So the *mechanism* the read named is
still unmeasured; only its *conclusion* has been falsified.

**And the forecast's second clause is now live against the print.** "FY26 base ₫30.5tn" implies FY
PBT ₫38,125bn. H1 PBT is **₫20,188bn**, so the base branch requires H2 to **fall 11.2%** and the bear
branch to fall 26.6%, against a market whose bank profits are seasonally second-half weighted.
**That is not a miss on a forecast, it is a scenario tree that has stopped discriminating** — which
is exactly what the 28-Jul pre-registered H2 table said the ≥₫20,000bn row would mean. Escalated for
a human rebuild; branch re-derivation is not an automated edit.

**Scored 1 hit, 3 misses across four resolved forecasts.**

## Resolution rules, pre-registered 2026-07-29 03:53 — before the numbers land

Four forecasts resolve within days. Three of them **cannot be scored as written**, and
fixing that after the print would let the scoring be shaped by the answer. Rules below are
fixed now.

### #1 · KDH — the forecast as written is close to unfalsifiable

It predicts Q2 parent NPAT "between MBS ₫170bn and SSI ₫348bn." **That range spans 105% —
it is the entire width of published estimates.** Predicting that the answer falls somewhere
between the published estimates is not a forecast, and it must not score as a hit.

**Scored instead on two things that are genuinely falsifiable:**
- the **FY26 base case of ₫1,590bn**, which is a point estimate, when the year resolves;
- the **pre-registered units read** in `dossiers/KDH.md` §3, which maps a Q2 print to units
  handed (₫170bn ⇒ ~19 units, ₫259bn ⇒ ~27, ₫348bn ⇒ ~35) and is checkable against the
  Gladia inventory movement.

**The range itself is recorded as a process failure regardless of where the print lands.**
Writing down the consensus span and calling it a forecast is the habit this log exists to
catch.

**Attribution corrected 2026-07-29 04:53.** The range is right but one label was wrong: **MBS is
at ₫170bn**, **SSI is at ₫330bn** (not 348), and the **₫348bn** figure belongs to a third house,
possibly VCBS, unconfirmed. Score against the range as stated, but do not attribute the top end
to SSI. See `assumptions.json` → `kdh.actuals._FORECAST_ATTRIBUTION_CORRECTED_2026_07_29`, which
also records that the ₫348bn forecast's own revenue implies ~26 units against the ~35 our
pre-registered read maps to that profit — a disagreement to resolve from the statement, not from
our mapping.

### #2 · HPG — already exact, no change

Core NPAT below **₫5,600bn** on the disclosed 3.5m-tonne basket, which is below ₫1.60m per
tonne. Threshold fixed 28 July. Scoreable as it stands.

### #4 · MBB — needs a band

Written as "Q2/26 NPAT ≈ ₫7,052bn." "Approximately" is not scoreable. The figure cross-checks
correctly against the other estimate on file: ₫8,812bn pre-tax × 0.80 = ₫7,050bn.

**Rule: hit if the actual lands within ±10%, i.e. ₫6,347–7,757bn.** Outside that is a miss,
and the signed error gets recorded either way. Ten percent is chosen because it is roughly
the dispersion between broker estimates on this name — a tighter band would score noise, a
wider one would score nothing.

### #10 · Rebar — "no thesis change" needs a test

Written as "headline risk only, <3% of revenue, no thesis change." The first two clauses are
checkable; the third is not, as written.

**Rule: the forecast holds if all three are true.** The final antidumping rate on Hoa Phat is
within ±20pp of the 121.97% preliminary; the scope is not widened beyond rebar; and US rebar
remains under 3% of HPG revenue. **If the rate rises materially or the scope expands, it is a
miss** — and note that Commerce has preliminarily treated Dung Quat, Hai Duong, Hung Yen and
Prestressed Concrete as a **single entity**, so the rate applies group-wide rather than to one
subsidiary.

**One thing this cannot resolve.** `federalregister.gov` returns proxy 403 for both the site
and its API, so the determination will be read from press rather than the register. Score the
direction when it lands; treat the exact rate as T5 until a human confirms it from the source.


## Standing lessons

_Populated as patterns emerge across scored forecasts. Seeded with errors already
made and caught — they were real and they are worth not repeating._

| Date | Lesson | Where it changed the process |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-24 | Aggregating percentage P&L across positions instead of value-weighting understated the drawdown by ~5pp (−13.9% reported vs −18.8% actual). | Portfolio math now computed from cost and value, never from averaging percentages |
| 2026-07-24 | Assumed KDH's Gladia JV was equity-accounted; the Q1 statements showed full consolidation. A structural assumption was made without reading the note. | Ownership/consolidation basis is now read from the filing before any model is built |
| 2026-07-26 | Derived TTM earnings from estimated share counts; MBB's base came out ~35% low and produced a fictitious +85% expected return. | `npat_ttm` is now derived from the earnings path (H1 actual + prior H2), with a market-cap cross-check |
| 2026-07-26 | A load-bearing input (`npat_ttm`) can be wrong for a year without anything catching it, because nothing recomputes it. VCI's was 25.2% off and it flipped the sign of expected return. | The cross-check the method already mandates is now run whenever a name is screened, not only when prices refresh |
| 2026-07-26 | "+36% YoY" and "−26% QoQ" described the same VCI quarter. Every source reported the first. | Sequential change is computed alongside year-on-year for every earnings datapoint; a base effect is not a trend |
| 2026-07-27 | **Same error twice in two days.** VCI's `npat_ttm` was 25.2% low; VPX's is ~9-27% low, and VPX was ranked #1 on expected return because of it. Both are the two names whose TTM bases were set as rough early estimates rather than derived. A one-off is a mistake; twice in the same field is a method failure. | Every `npat_ttm` gets the mandated cross-check before it can drive a recommendation — not only when a name happens to be screened. TCX was control-checked and is fine, so the defect is bounded to VCI and VPX |
| 2026-07-27 | Logged a Fitch **forecast** for iron ore (US$115/t) as though it were the **spot price**, then built a cost bridge on it. Spot was US$98–104 and is US$97.70 today — ore never rose. The error made HPG's economics look near-breakeven when they were not. | Charter §3 already requires checking "filed actual / guidance / broker estimate" — that check now applies to **commodity prices too**, not just company earnings. A forecast and a price are different objects |
| 2026-07-26 | Applied a *peak* exit multiple to *peak* cyclical earnings on HPG, and to a non-repeating divestment gain. | Cyclical multiples are now inverted (low on peak, high on trough) and one-offs stripped before any multiple is applied |
| 2026-07-28 | A scheduled **operating disclosure** — HPG's half-year sales volumes — sat unlogged for about three weeks while hourly sweeps ran. The lanes hunt for *news*; nobody was watching the *calendar* of routine company releases, which are tier-T2 primary data and often more useful than the press coverage that follows them. | Recurring operating releases (monthly and quarterly volume/output disclosures) are now treated as dated catalysts to be checked on their schedule, not as news to be stumbled upon |
| 2026-07-28 | **Third input in three days to fail the period/entity checks.** VCI and VPX were both `npat_ttm`; VPB's is a credit balance. Because the third one is a different field, the pattern is not about `npat_ttm` at all — it is about numbers transcribed out of press summaries without pinning down which period and which entity they describe. VPB's block held a Q1-consolidated balance and an H1-parent growth rate side by side as if they were one quarter of one company, and the error was invisible until the two were divided into each other. | Any two fields in the same block that can be arithmetically checked against each other must be, at the time of entry. `credit_q2 / credit_start_fy - 1` had to equal `credit_growth_ytd` and never did |
| 2026-07-28 | **A single sentence contradicted itself and stood for a day.** The 27-Jul coking-coal note said 'SPOT 228, -24% MoM from 238.9 on 10-Jul' - but 238.9 to 228 is -4.6%. The consistency audit run that same morning checked FIELDS against each other and would never have caught this, because both numbers and the false percentage linking them sat inside one prose string. The error mattered: it produced a 'coal is collapsing, this is upside risk' reading when coal was in fact flat, and the corrected spread at spot is 0.60-0.68m/t rather than 0.83m/t - further below the bear branch, not closer. | Percentages stated inside prose notes get recomputed from the figures in the same sentence. A number written as narrative is still a number and gets the same arithmetic check as one written as a field |
| 2026-07-28 | **A near-miss of a different kind: nearly logged an unverified claim because it agreed with me.** A search summary said KDH's Gladia handover pace was 'slower than originally expected'. | 2026-08-03 | **A percentage pair filed by a company is a share count, and this file held one for days without dividing it.** KDH's insider disclosure — *"0.056% → 1.838% by buying 20,000,000 shares"* — implies **1,122,334,456 shares outstanding**, confirming to **0.024%** a count derived hours earlier from charter capital and flagged then as *"a plan with a closed window, not a completion notice… one completion notice settles it."* It also **rejects** both rival counts by ~0.2pp. The pair had sat in the date-gate table for days, read only as a signal about insider conviction. | **A disclosed percentage is a measurement of the denominator, not just the numerator.** Any filing that states a stake before and after a known share transaction encodes the total outstanding, and companies compute those against the real register — making it a primary-source share count available without a search. **Add to the standing checks: whenever a stake-change disclosure gives two percentages and a share quantity, divide.** Note the shape, which is now the third instance: `pe_ttm × npat_ttm`, the 0.056%/1.838% pair, and MBB's provisions as TOI − opex − PBT were all **recoverable from figures already on file and were not recovered, because the routines were built to find news rather than to interrogate what was already held.** |

Logging it would have recorded the market at an all-time high in a file that correctly has it below its 50-week average and falling for four straight weeks. **The trap arrived bundled with a true fact** — the Moody's upgrade is real, just three months old. | **A "record high" that equals a number already stored as a past peak is a period flag by construction, and the check costs nothing because the comparison value is already in the file.** This is the second stale market article in three days and both were caught by stored values rather than by further searching. **Generalise it: any superlative — record, all-time, highest since — gets compared against the file's own stored extreme before it is believed.** Note the compounding danger: **stale articles bundle true and false claims**, so verifying one element ("did Moody's upgrade Vietnam? yes") can license the rest of the paragraph unless each claim is dated separately. |
| 2026-08-03 | **Found the three banks separated by a rating agency and stopped short of reading the separation as a verdict.** Moody's raised six Vietnamese banks to a positive outlook on 5-May-2026 — Vietcombank, BIDV, Agribank, VietinBank, ACB and **VPBank** — with **TCB and MBB absent**. The tempting inference is that TCB and MBB were assessed and found wanting, which would sit neatly beside TCB's Group-2 loans +46% and its developer concentration. **It was recorded as not established**: absence from a list of six is not evidence of review, and Moody's may not rate them at all. | **An exclusion is not a finding until you know the population that was considered.** A list of six upgraded banks tells you about six banks; it tells you nothing about a seventh unless the seventh was in scope. This is the same discipline that held eight hours earlier on VPB's provisioning, where a strong three-name pattern would have predicted the wrong sign. **The tell for this failure mode is that the inference would have been convenient** — it fits an existing negative thesis on TCB, which by the 28-Jul lesson is a reason to verify harder, not to relax. |

Debt is at a record ₫98,530bn, up 9% in six months, with financial costs roughly doubled. | **A caveat carried in prose for days is a measurement that was never taken.** The pattern is now explicit across this file: `cap_now`, the 0.056%/1.838% pair, MBB's provisions and this were all *known to be uncertain* and none were *quantified* until something forced it. **Operationally: when a note says a model omits something, the same sweep computes the magnitude — an omission whose size is unknown cannot be ranked against any other open item**, which is precisely why this one sat below cosmetic items for a week. Note the discipline that survived the sizing: **NPAT is struck after interest, so this is not double-counting or an arithmetic hole** — the defect is that no branch varies with it, which is a smaller and more precise claim than "the model ignores interest". |

The common cause is now clear enough to name: **findings are filed under the sweep that produced them, and open questions live in a separate index, with nothing that re-reads one against the other.** Concretely: **when a sweep records any new figure about a holding, it greps OPEN-DECISIONS for that holding before closing** — the cost is one search of a local file, and it would have caught all four. |

| 2026-08-03 | **The index file that exists so findings can be found had two items numbered 19 and two numbered 20.** §1/§4 carry MBB's branches and KDH's land-use fee; the CFA section carries λ and volatility-scaled corridors. **Log entries and commit messages cite "item 19" and "item 20" ambiguously.** Fixed by **disambiguation, not renumbering** — the log and git history are append-only and already cite the numbers, so renumbering would silently invalidate live references. | **In an append-only record, identifiers are permanent even when they are wrong, and the fix for a bad identifier is annotation rather than reassignment.** Renumbering would have made every historical citation quietly point at the wrong item — a worse failure than the collision, and undetectable. **Standing rule: new items number from the current maximum upward across the whole file, never per-section**, which is how the collision arose in the first place when a new section restarted at 19. |

Zero repo hits for *"soát xét"* or *"bán niên"*. **The file thought its next filing gate was 88 days away; it is 11.** The routine would have reproduced that error every week. | **Copying a source into a control surface copies its errors and then hides them behind a schedule.** OPEN-DECISIONS item 12 already warns that duplicated rule-copies *drift* from the repo; **this copy did not drift — it was born wrong**, which is worse, because drift is detectable by comparison and an inherited error is not. **Standing rule: when a routine's prompt is written from the repo, the source is verified against the primary regulation first, not transcribed.** Note what caught it: the routine's own STEP 1 asked for a filing-calendar check, and running that check honestly against the regulation rather than against the file is what exposed it. **A control that audits its own premises is worth more than one that executes them.** |

The file already held `q2_financial_income` at **₫906bn** — a 1% match — so **the two are the same money seen two ways**, and each confirms the other. | **When a newly itemised total lands within rounding of a figure already on file, that is corroboration in both directions and should be stated as such** — it raises confidence in the new detail *and* retrospectively validates the old aggregate. Separately, the CPI direction claim withdrawn at 19:53 the previous evening — *"accelerating"* replaced with *"unknown"* — was pointed the same way by the PMI release's report of **easing inflationary pressure**. | **Corroboration counts only when the routes are independent, and this file has previously been burned by the opposite** — the 30-Jul KDH failure counted one press phrasing three times and called it "three routes agree". **The discriminator is whether the second observation could have come out differently given the first.** FDI disbursement and a purchasing-managers' survey are separately sourced and separately timed, so their agreement is informative; three paraphrases of one article are not. **Worth recording as the positive case, since the log is otherwise dense with the failure mode.** |

The print was logged as supportive of volume and **explicitly barred from relieving the spread case**. | **A supportive fact that lands on a non-binding variable is not partial support; it is no support for the thing at issue.** The temptation is to let good news soften a bear case somewhere in the vicinity — the 26-Jul spread evidence, the August price cuts and the interest step all concern margin per tonne, and none is touched by demand strength. **State which variable a new fact reaches, and say plainly when that is not the one under argument.** |

May is confirmed as the peak.** And the 7-month **cumulative average rose 4.38% → 4.39% in the same month.** | **The direction call is the cheap half; the clause about the average is the half that would have prevented the original error from recurring.** Had the withdrawal stopped at replacing "accelerating" with "unknown", the July release — whose headline framing leads with the rising 7-month average — would have re-produced the identical misreading on fresh data. **A withdrawn conclusion stays withdrawn only if the method that generated it is withdrawn too, and the way to make that stick is to pre-commit, in writing, to what the next data point may NOT be judged by.** That is a cheaper and more reliable device than resolving to be careful. **Generalise: whenever a claim is withdrawn for a measurement error rather than a factual one, name the specific number that caused it and bar it from the next reading by name.** |

Separately, the print **weakens** the 31-Jul "SBV is boxed in on two sides" conclusion by loosening the measured side and leaving the inferred FX side alone — recorded as a downgrade. | **A verified prediction is the moment this file is least likely to look for the disconfirming sub-series, because the top-line already agreed with it.** Headline CPI and core CPI are different measures of different things; the one that moved is the volatile one, which mean-reverts with the oil price. **Also: when a conclusion was explicitly built on two supports and new data removes one, the conclusion does not merely survive — it survives WEAKER, and if that is not written at the moment it happens, nobody re-derives it later.** The 31-Jul entry itself flagged which support was measured and which was inferred; **that labelling is what made this re-scoring possible three days on, and it is why supports should be labelled by evidence type when they are first laid down.** |

Fitch's iron ore assumption is US$100, not the US$115 in this file** — so the coal input was exact and the ore input was invented. Corrected, ore is 1.75% below forecast and coal 0.7%, and both sit *on* the source. The bridge's recommended downside branch moves from ₫0.36m/t to ₫0.86m/t — **2.4×**, on 16.8% of the book. | **When two inputs share a source, the SPREAD BETWEEN THEIR ERRORS is a test of the inputs, not a measurement of the world — and a large asymmetry is close to proof that one of them is wrong.** Nothing external is needed to run that test; it was available the moment both numbers were written in the same sentence, and it sat there for three days *already computed*. **This file has a standing rule to suspect the derivation before the data. The rule was applied to incoming figures and not to a figure already resident**, which is the same failure as the TCX share count on 03-Aug 09:53 with the direction reversed. **Also: a correction is not a clearance.** `_CORRECTED_2026_07_27` looked at the 115, identified it as mis-labelled, gave it a NEW label ("a Fitch forecast") and closed — **and the new label was never checked against Fitch.** A correction that substitutes one unverified provenance for another leaves the number more trusted than before, not less. |

For a week the file explained the gap between the bridge's ₫0.36m and the spot work's ₫0.86–1.10m as *forecast versus spot* — a plausible economic story for a discrepancy that was purely an input error. | **An unexplained gap between two of your own estimates will always attract a narrative, and the narrative is the danger, because a plausible one stops the search.** *Forecast versus spot* is exactly the kind of explanation that sounds like understanding: it is real, it is the right shape, and here it accounted for none of the difference. **Rule: when two internal estimates of the same quantity disagree, the reconciliation must be ARITHMETIC — name the inputs that differ and show the gearing that turns them into the gap — before any economic story is allowed to close it.** The gearing was in §2 of the bridge the whole time. **Corollary worth keeping: exact agreement between independently-derived numbers is strong evidence, and it only became visible once the wrong input was removed — which means a persistent discrepancy is itself an unexploited asset, not noise to be narrated away.** |

| 2026-08-04 | **Named a discriminator, then actually ran it one sweep later — and it converted a refusal into a result.** At 03:53 I declined to explain a 5bp yield fall on a four-year-high ISM as *"growth without inflation"* and wrote down the test: *soft prices would permit it, firm prices would not*. **ISM Prices Paid printed 71.1 — the 22nd consecutive month of rising input costs.** The narrative is not unsupported, it is **contradicted**. | **A named discriminator is worth little until someone runs it, and the gap between naming and running is where most "open questions" in this file quietly die.** What made this one cheap was that the test was stated as a *specific figure with a specific direction* — one search resolved it. **Compare the questions on this page that have sat open for a week: every one of them names a topic rather than a number.** The other half of the lesson: **refusing a plausible narrative bought the option to test it later, and the option paid.** Had I written the story at 03:53 I would now be unwinding it instead of collecting the result. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Narrowed a withdrawal I had made an hour earlier, on data that happened to favour me — and flagged the move as suspicious in the same breath.** At 03:53 I withdrew *"ISM restores the FX support"* because rates did not reprice hawkishly. **What was falsified was the MARKET-REACTION claim; the FUNDAMENTAL claim — an economy with 22 months of rising input prices does not get a rate cut — was untouched and is now better evidenced.** | **What the data implies and what the market priced are different claims, and I bundled them, so one refutation took down both.** Same species as the level-versus-direction lesson filed an hour earlier, one layer up: **a fact about the world and a prediction about prices need separate sentences and separate evidence.** **But the more important part is the procedure, because reinstating a withdrawn claim on convenient new data is the single most self-serving move available**: the safeguards used were that the reinstatement is STRICTLY NARROWER than the original and rests on a DIFFERENT EVIDENCE TYPE, and that the entry says plainly that if a reader disagrees, the withdrawal is the safe reading. **A reinstatement that cannot survive being labelled a reinstatement should not be made.** |
| 2026-08-04 | **Recorded a 54bp move as a finding without ever recording the range of the series it came from.** The 31-Jul entry made VND interbank a finding — overnight 2.43% → 2.97% across July, *"funding cost moved and the file had no record of it"*. **Two figures surfaced since put the same rate near 1% in a late-July session and above 7% in a May week.** If the series swings 1–7%, **a 54bp move in a monthly average is small against its own scale.** | **A move is only a signal against a scale, and I recorded the move without the scale — which is the difference between noticing a number changed and knowing whether the change means anything.** The specific trap: **a monthly AVERAGE and a session PRINT are different quantities**, so what looked like a flat contradiction (2.97% vs 1%) is probably compatible — and the resolution and the qualification are the same fact. **Rule: before treating a change in a time series as informative, get one number for its dispersion.** One extra figure would have set the 31-Jul entry's confidence correctly at the time. **Note also what saved this: that entry recorded its own single-sourcing ("both returns trace to the same VietnamNet article... one T5 route, not two"), which is precisely why it could be qualified rather than defended.** A finding that names its weakest link stays revisable. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Two of eight share counts are now externally verified and the unverified one is the engine's largest conviction.** VCI was confirmed to 0.07% by an insider filing and TCX by HOSE's own VN30 cap disclosure — **TCX being the name where the stale-count error actually happened.** MBB, rank 1 with a +6.7pp proposed ADD, rests on charter capital ÷ par alone. **Plausibility checks (P/B 1.20× vs 0.91×, P/E 5.76 vs 4.36) fit BOTH the current and a pre-stock-dividend count and settle nothing.** | **Verification effort followed where the error was found rather than where the exposure is largest, which is the natural order and the wrong one.** TCX got three independent confirmations because it had failed; MBB got none because it had not. **After a defect is fixed, the next thing to check is not the name that broke — it is the name with the most riding on the same mechanism.** The second half matters as much: **`cap_now` equals shares × price BY CONSTRUCTION, so internal consistency can never detect a stale count** — the TCX error passed that check while being 20% wrong. **Where a check is structurally incapable of failing, absence of failure must not be logged as reassurance**, which is the same lesson as the dead market-cap cross-check, reaching a different quantity. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Sized a hazard before it happened rather than after, and the sizing is what makes it actionable.** MBB has a 15% stock dividend and a 10:1 rights issue pending. A stock dividend is value-neutral because the price adjusts on the ex-date, **so the risk is applying one leg without the other: shares-only takes E[r] +36.6% → +18.8%, price-only takes it → +57.0%. Both exceed the 10pp escalation threshold; both legs together leave it unchanged.** | **A warning without a number is advice; a warning with a number is a specification.** "Watch out for corporate actions" has been on file for TCX since 02-Aug and did not stop the TCX share-count error four days later. **What changes behaviour is the pair of magnitudes and the fact that the two errors point in OPPOSITE directions — which means neither a sanity check on sign nor a plausibility check on level would catch them.** **Filed against the action that will trigger it (item 2's price refresh) rather than against the name**, because the error will be made by whoever executes that item, not by whoever reads MBB's dossier. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Named a verification gap at 07:53 and closed it at 08:53 — and closing it enlarged the problem rather than retiring it.** MB's disclosed statutory capital is *"nearly ₫80,550bn"*, exactly the 8,055m × ₫10,000 par on file, **and three separate tranche figures in the same disclosure divide back to the same base.** But the plan also contains a **private placement of up to 200m shares the watch list does not track**, so the pending increase is **+27.5%, not +15%**, and the one-legged refresh error grows from −17.8pp to **−29.4pp**. | **Verification is not the same as reassurance: the check passed and the exposure went up, because reading the document properly surfaced a tranche the summary version had dropped.** The watch list said *"15% stock dividend + 10:1 rights"* — a compressed description that had been accurate enough to repeat and was never re-derived from the source. **A second-hand summary of a corporate action decays silently, because nothing about it looks stale.** The general form: **when a check confirms a number, read what else the confirming document says** — the confirmation was one line and the omitted tranche was two lines below it. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Nearly inferred an issue price from a charter-capital figure.** The placement reads *"up to 200 million shares, adding approximately ₫2,000bn in capital"* — and 200m × ₫10,000 **par** = ₫2,000bn exactly, so **that number is the charter-capital effect, not the proceeds**, and says nothing about what the shares will be sold for. The rights tranche, by contrast, states its price explicitly. | **In Vietnamese capital-raise disclosures, charter capital always moves by par × shares regardless of issue price, so any "adds ₫X bn in capital" figure is a share-count statement in currency clothing.** Treating it as proceeds would have implied a placement at par — a 54.6% discount to market — and produced a large fictitious value transfer. **The tell was that the number divided exactly by par**, which is the same technique that recovered share counts from KDH's and VCI's ownership percentages: *a figure that divides exactly by a structural constant is probably that constant times a count, not an independent measurement.* Third instance of that technique paying, and the first where it prevented an error rather than found one. |
| 2026-08-04 | **The same par-value trap appeared twice in two sweeps, and the second instance was inside the routine's own watch list.** MBB's placement was described as *"adding ~₫2,000bn in capital"* (= 200m × par); VPB's is carried by the routine as a **"$250m foreign placement"** — and 624m × ₫10,000 par = ₫6,240bn ≈ **US$237m**. **Both figures are charter-capital effects, not money raised.** At the file's price VPB's same 624m shares would be ~US$593m. | **In Vietnamese capital-raise disclosures charter capital moves by par × shares regardless of issue price, so any "adds ₫X bn in capital" figure is a share-count statement in currency clothing** — and once converted to dollars it stops looking like one entirely. **The tell both times was that the figure divided exactly by par**, the same technique that recovered share counts from KDH's and VCI's ownership percentages and from VPB's dividend total. **What makes the second instance worse is where it lived: the watch list I read every sweep.** A compressed description in a standing instruction is repeated, not re-derived, so an error there is quoted indefinitely — and I had read "$250m" dozens of times without dividing it by anything. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Ran a check on the name that broke, then on the name with the most riding on it, then on the name with the same defect and more weight — and only the third order was right.** After the TCX share-count error, verification went TCX → MBB (rank 1) → VPB. **VPB turns out to carry the LARGER pending increase (+33.9% vs +27.5%) on the LARGER weight (10.0% vs 6.5%).** | **"Check the name with the most riding on the mechanism" was yesterday's rule and it still under-selected, because it ranked by E[r] position rather than by EXPOSURE TO THE DEFECT.** MBB was rank 1, so it felt like the priority; VPB was rank 8 and had a third more dilution pending. **When a defect class is identified, enumerate every name that could carry it BEFORE ranking which to check — the enumeration is cheap and the ranking is where the bias enters.** Four of eight are now externally confirmed and all four passed, which is the argument for finishing the sweep rather than stopping at the interesting ones. |

| 2026-08-04 | **One report gave three values for the same quantity; the two that agreed had been derived by different routes, and the outlier was the prose.** TCB's coverage says the 60% bonus issues *"hơn 4,28 tỷ"* shares AND raises charter capital by **₫42,517bn** — but 4,280m × par = ₫42,800bn, a ₫283bn contradiction inside one article. **₫42,517bn ÷ par = 4,251.7m = exactly 60% of the file's count, and the cash-dividend route (₫4,960bn ÷ ₫700) independently gives the same base.** | **Agreement is only evidence when the routes are independent — the standing rule — and here it discriminated between three figures in a single source rather than between sources.** The two structural numbers (a cash total, a charter-capital increase) are audited quantities that must reconcile; the share count in prose is a rounded restatement and reconciles to nothing. **Prefer the figure that is forced to tie to something else over the figure that merely describes.** Worth noting the near-miss: *"over 4.28 billion"* is the number a reader would quote, and it is the only one of the three that is wrong. |

It was never turned on KDH's own `shares_outstanding`**, which it confirms to 0.0245% with one division and no search. | **A lesson extracted from a datum can travel outward while the datum itself stays unexploited at home** — the generalisation felt like the payoff, so the local application was skipped. **Rule: when a datum yields a reusable method, apply the method to the datum's own subject FIRST, because that is the one case where you already have every input.** Seventh recovery of a quantity from data already held and the cheapest of them all; it cost one line of arithmetic against three days of it sitting there. |

I had the right quote and drew the wrong boundary from it. **The aggravating factor is that the previous entry congratulated the enumeration for producing a tighter bound**, which is exactly when a bound stops being questioned: **a satisfying conclusion is the least likely one to get a second reading**, and this one was two hours old and already copied into two other files. **Tightening a scope is a claim like any other and needs the same adversarial pass as an expansion — arguably more, because the reward for narrowing is felt immediately and the cost is felt later.** |

This is the same trap running forward, and the check that caught it was the strongest available: the number matched a stored value for a DIFFERENT DATE.** An exact match to something you already hold is not corroboration, it is a fingerprint. **Generalise: when a new figure equals a stored figure exactly, the first hypothesis is not "nothing changed" but "this is the stored observation coming back with a new date on it."** A file without memory would have adopted it, and would have recorded a flat session as a repeat of a strong one. |

Weighted, the five understate book E[r] by 1.04pp against a reported +1.8% — about 58% of it.** | **The assertion overstated the COUNT and nobody had ever computed the SIZE — which is the usual shape of an inherited caveat: directionally right, quantitatively unexamined, and repeated for a week because it sounded prudent.** A caveat that names a number gets checked; one that names a direction gets quoted. **And the size is what makes it actionable: 1.04pp on a book expected to return 1.8% is not a footnote, it is most of the signal.** Note also the sequencing failure this exposes — MBB's 4.54% was already sitting *inside the same risk.py paragraph* that called it a blank, so one of the seven had been measured all along by the very note complaining they were not. |

And the timing sharpens it: this surfaced only because I audited the table instead of searching, on an evening when searching would have returned nothing. |

| 2026-08-04 | **Iron ore fell 4.43% in a day and I did not report it as relief, because this file made exactly that mistake one week ago.** On 29-Jul it read ore −5.3% and coal −2.2% as *"both cost blades easing, which is the one thing that would relieve HPG's H2 spread squeeze"* and corrected it on 30-Jul when Vietnamese mills were found **raising** prices on anchored inputs. **Ore falls when steel margins compress and output is cut, so a falling cost blade can be a symptom of a falling revenue blade.** | **A logged failure earns its cost only when it fires on a case that arrives looking like good news**, and this one did: the modelled spot case improves ₫0.86m/t → ~₫1.08m/t, the closest it has come to any branch since the bridge was built. **The tell that stopped it was structural rather than economic — only ONE of the three legs moved**, and holding the revenue blade fixed while the cost blade falls is the same shape as the one-legged price refresh this file spent all day warning about on four bank names. **Two unrelated workstreams producing the same error shape is a reason to treat it as a general defect, not a local one.** |
| 2026-08-05 | **Named a discriminator, ran it, and recorded that it could not be resolved rather than using the near-miss that came back.** I asked for an HRC print dated **after 31-Jul** to separate a cost windfall from a demand signal in the 03-Aug ore fall. **What returned was 24-July data that the search summary framed as "early August 2026."** | **Tenth period trap, and the second in two days where the summariser — not the source — attached the wrong date.** The sources said 24-July plainly. **The specific discipline: a test with a stated date requirement is either met or not met, and "close enough" data fails it by definition** — the whole point of the requirement was that the ore move happened *after* the window this data covers. **Recording a test as unresolved is the second time in three days I have had to resist substituting adjacent evidence** (the other: the 10-year yield at 02:53 on 04-Aug, also pre-dating the event it would have explained). **The substitute always arrives already pointing the way you wanted, which is what makes it feel like an answer.** |

This is the same error at a scale small enough to survive a sanity check**, which is exactly why the rule has to be applied mechanically rather than when something looks wrong. **A 10% gap and a 5% gap support different sentences about competitive pressure**, and the difference between them is entirely freight. **Generalise: before comparing two prices, state the delivery terms of each; if they differ, adjust or refuse the comparison — never quote the raw difference with a caveat attached, because the number travels and the caveat does not.** |
| 2026-08-05 | **Turned a two-hour-old rule on my own model instead of on incoming data, and it found a structural gap.** At 00:53 I wrote *"before comparing two prices, state the delivery terms of each"* and applied it to a Chinese FOB quote. **The HPG bridge uses three prices — ore, coking coal and HRC — and not one of them carries a delivery basis anywhere in the file**, while the file separately holds Indian HRC *landed* and China HRC *FOB*. | **This file's standing failure, logged 03-Aug, is that a lesson applied only to incoming data isn't applied — and I had just done it again.** The rule was written about a search result and the model it most obviously indicts was sitting one file away. **A rule aimed outward is half a rule; the same test run against your own inputs is where it costs something.** Note also the enabling condition: this was found during a dead window with both markets shut, which is the second time in four hours that having nothing to search produced a better result than searching would have. |
| 2026-08-05 | **The solved conversion cost hides the defect it absorbs.** The bridge's US$114/t is *calibrated* so the model reproduces the known Q1 actual of ₫1.68m/t — **so it silently absorbs whatever basis mismatch existed at Q1 prices, and stays valid only while the basis spreads hold constant.** A freight move would enter the model as an apparent change in conversion cost. **At ₫0.021m/t per US$1/t of HRC, an US$8.3/t freight move alone covers the ₫0.174m gap between the current spot case and the bear branch.** | **A solved parameter is a residual, and a residual absorbs every error you did not model — which makes the model fit better and see less.** The calibration was recorded as a strength (*"conversion cost is solved, not guessed"*) and it is one, but the same property that anchors it to a real observation makes it a catch-all for unmodelled bases. **Rule: whenever a parameter is solved rather than measured, write down what it is absorbing — the list is the model's blind spot, stated in advance.** And size it against the distance the model is currently being read over, not against the parameter itself. |
| 2026-08-05 | **A number the file has printed all along was being read as a preference when it is a diagnostic.** `cfa.py` computes **λ_implied = −0.209** and its prose calls that *"barely risk-averse at all"* and offers *"somewhere between −0.21 and 6.0"* as the plausible range. **A negative λ is risk-SEEKING: the book expects +1.8% against a 3.56% government bond while carrying 29.2% vol — an excess return of −1.79%.** | **The sign was doing all the work and the prose read only the magnitude.** "Barely risk-averse" and "risk-seeking" are different claims, and only one of them is a statement about preferences at all — **no risk-averse investor holds that book, so −0.21 is not a lower bound on a preference, it is evidence the inputs are broken.** **Generalise from the 02:53 rule: a solved parameter is a residual, so when its value falls outside the range its interpretation permits, that is the residual reporting a defect, not a discovery about the thing being measured.** The failure mode is subtle because the number was computed correctly, printed prominently, and simply described in the wrong category. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Qualified my own "cheapest improvement available" finding from nine hours earlier: it does not close the gap.** Restoring the 1.04pp `cash_yield` omission moves book E[r] 1.8% → 2.84% and λ_implied −0.206 → **−0.084 — still negative.** The book needs **+1.76pp** merely to reach risk-neutral indifference with the 2Y bond. | **A fix being the cheapest available and a fix being sufficient are different claims, and I made the first while implying the second.** The 18:53 entry called 1.04pp "about 58% of the reported book E[r]", which is true and makes it sound decisive; **measured against the RIGHT benchmark — the risk-free rate, not the reported E[r] — it covers 59% of the shortfall and leaves the sign unchanged.** **Choosing the denominator chooses the conclusion, and I picked the flattering one without noticing.** The useful by-product: **λ_implied now serves as a free progress metric** — nothing in this repo tracked aggregate model health, and a single scalar that sums every known input defect does it for nothing. |

Here the resolution was that they measure different moments, not different things.** The tell was the same either way: **what each figure is attached to** — a session-progress phrase versus a full curve snapshot. **A measure check is not a rejection procedure; it is a procedure for finding out what each number is, and sometimes the answer is that both belong.** Worth recording because the habit of discarding the odd one out would have thrown away the intraday figure that confirms the stored value. |

| 2026-08-05 | **A fact recorded in anonymised form read as complete and stopped the search that would have named it.** The file has carried *"18 projects of 3 private economic groups excluded from the credit room"* since it was logged. **The three groups are Vingroup, Sun Group and Masterise — one of them is the subject of an armed kill criterion on the largest position in the book — and the names were in Vietnamese headlines on 23-Jun.** | **This is the third instance of the gazette-blindness pattern (Circular 25, HPG's half-year volumes) but a worse species: in those two the file held NOTHING, and an absence is visible.** Here it held a partial record wearing the grammar of a finished one — *"three private economic groups"* is a phrase that parses as a fact, so nothing in it asked to be followed up. **Rule: an anonymised quantity in a file whose every other counterparty is named is a missing search in disguise; treat "N unnamed entities" as an open item, not as a datum.** The cost of the omission was not the names themselves but the two things that came with them — the exempted **single-customer credit limit**, and a Masterise entity that is an *aviation-infrastructure* company. |

The file's T1 entry on Circular 25 says it amends **Circular 22/2019 on prudential limits** — and credit-room allocation is not made under Circular 22. **The vehicle is Công văn 5386/NHNN-TD, dated 22-Jun-2026 — the same day Circular 25 was issued**, which is why the two merge in press summaries. | **A well-specified prior entry is a detector, and this is the second time this week one has fired** (the other being the 03-Aug session-date catch, where a stored value contradicted a presented date). The tell was not that the claim sounded wrong — it sounded entirely plausible — but that the file already recorded what that instrument *does*, and the two did not fit. **Rule: when incoming information attributes an action to a named instrument the file already holds, check the attribution against the file's description of the instrument before recording the action.** And the distinction earned by catching it is not cosmetic: **a thông tư is normative law, a công văn is administrative guidance** — the carve-out is far more revocable than the circular framing implied. |

| 2026-08-05 | **A date gate I created suppressed the search for an event that had already happened.** The row written 04-Aug 22:53 said the SBV credit print was *"DUE ~05-AUG, TOMORROW"*, derived from a **"month-end +5d"** rule. **It was published 03-Aug at the regular government press conference — ₫20,150,000bn at 29-Jul, +8.38%.** | **Date gates exist to stop wasted searching, so a wrong one is not a neutral error — it is an instruction not to look, and it is obeyed.** The rule was inferred from how statistical agencies publish; **SBV credit numbers come out through the government press-conference cycle, which follows a political calendar, not a statistical one.** The 03-Aug sweeps were busy with the CPI/PMI cluster and this landed in the same cycle, unlooked-for. **Rule: a gate must record the CHANNEL an item publishes through, not just an inferred date — and a gate built from a rule rather than an observed release should be marked as a guess.** Note the asymmetry that makes this expensive: a gate set too EARLY costs one wasted search; a gate set too LATE costs every sweep until it expires. |

The coverage describes it as the share of Treasury money **placed at** commercial banks — a liquidity injection. **Same 20→50, same 01-Aug-2026→31-Jul-2028 window, so plainly the same instrument.** | **Matching numbers authenticated the instrument and concealed that the mechanism was in dispute** — the window and the ratio agreed exactly, which is precisely what made it feel settled enough to dismiss as a duplicate. **The file's own entry had already flagged that it could not establish whether this amends Circular 08 or stands alone; the honest update is that the uncertainty got SHARPER, not that it was resolved either way.** **Rule: when a duplicate is detected by matching figures, check that the two accounts describe the same MECHANISM before filing it as a duplicate — identical numbers are consistent with two incompatible stories, and the effect sizes here differ by orders of magnitude.** |
| 2026-08-05 | **Ran the arithmetic on a headline that reads as acceleration and it says deceleration.** *"Credit +8.38%, past ₫20.15 quadrillion"* against end-Jun +7.73% and 13-Jul +7.86%: **H1 ran 104% of the pace a 15% year needs; full July ran 55%; the remainder now requires 1.91× July's actual pace.** | **The headline number was up, the level was a record, and the derivative was the only thing that carried information — and it pointed the other way.** Two rate points were needed to see it and both were already on file. **Generalise: for any cumulative-YTD series, the reportable quantity is the PERIOD increment against the pace the target implies, never the cumulative figure the source leads with.** **And the discipline that mattered more than the finding: Vietnamese credit is Q4-weighted, this file holds no prior-year monthly path, so "behind pace" is arithmetic and not a forecast miss — stating the limit is what stops a true calculation from becoming a false conclusion.** |
| 2026-08-05 | **A stated expectation had an unstated assumption inside it, and the assumption was 100%.** OPEN-DECISIONS says two intermediate 1%-crossing disclosures *"should fire during"* VCI's Tô Hải window. **On 2.70pp of registered buying, crossing 16% needs 32.2% completion and 17% needs 69.3% — and the one measured completion rate available is 66.6%** (HPG: 33.3m of 50m registered, shortfall blamed explicitly on price). **The expectation fails by 2.7pp of completion.** | **A registration is an intention, and the file had been reading intentions as quantities.** Nothing in the sentence was wrong; what was missing is that it silently required near-full execution, so it could never have been scored — a prediction with a hidden free parameter is not falsifiable. **Rule: when an expectation depends on a threshold being crossed, compute the fraction of the announced action the threshold requires and state it — that fraction IS the forecast.** Note the asymmetry that makes shortfalls expensive to interpret: **a completed buy confirms the signal; a partial buy is consistent with conviction meeting a bad entry AND with conviction weakening, so it degrades the read rather than inverting it.** |

| 2026-08-05 | **A word that was in the file from the start had no date attached to it, and nobody noticed for three days.** `decision_612_qd_bct` records the measure as **"anti-CIRCUMVENTION duty, TEMPORARY, 27.83%"** along with 24 HS codes, four exempt grades and the width band. **The statutory clock says the investigation's ordinary 9-month deadline was 27-Jul-2026 — it lapsed nine days ago — with an outer bound of 27-Oct-2026, and Decision 612 states no term for the temporary duty itself.** | **Detail density read as completeness.** The entry is one of the most thoroughly specified in `_regulatory` — HS codes to two decimals, named exempt grades — and that precision is exactly what stopped anyone asking the crude question *"until when?"* **Rule: for any measure recorded as temporary, provisional, interim or pilot, the expiry is a REQUIRED field; if it cannot be established, record the absence explicitly rather than the adjective alone.** Note which half was harder: the statutory deadline came from one search; **the duty's own term is still unestablished and is the sharper risk, because a lapse before the final leaves an unprotected interval with the case still open.** |

| 2026-08-05 | **A standing back-sweep sat unrun for three days and answered its own first question on the first attempt.** The MoIT lane was created 02-Aug after Decision 612 was found **3½ months late**, with item (a) written as *"the FINAL determination on wide-format HRC — date unknown."* **Two searches produced the initiation date, the 9+3-month statutory limit, the lapsed ordinary deadline, the 27-Oct outer bound and a 03-Jul public hearing.** | **The cost of not running it was not the searches saved but the three days the file spent treating a dated, expiring measure as permanent.** A lane created in response to a miss is evidence that the miss is systematic, which is precisely why deferring it compounds. **Rule: a back-sweep opened because something was found late should be run on its FIRST eligible sweep, not merely scheduled — the schedule is what failed the first time.** It was deferred twice on the honest ground that Vietnamese sources were asleep; the honest ground was also the reason it never ran. |

Recorded as a FAILURE of the test's design.** At 11:53 I wrote that if TCBS's margin share of assets were *"materially lower"* than VPS's 65.4%, TCX's ROE gap would be **mix** and *"the premium partly defends itself."* **It is 51.2% — 14.2pp lower, condition met — and the premium is not defended: ROA is 5.65% vs 9.33%, and TCBS closes to within 1.9pp of VPS's ROE only by running 2.20× assets/equity against 1.54×.** | **The test measured composition when the question was productivity.** Margin share of assets explains *why* the assets yield less; it says nothing about whether a lower yield is acceptable — and I let a plausible-sounding intermediate variable stand in for the thing I actually wanted to know. **Rule: a pre-registered test must be stated as the DECOMPOSITION that would change the answer, not as a proxy that would merely be consistent with it — here the right pre-registration was ROE = ROA × leverage, which needed the same two searches and would have discriminated.** Note the failure mode is invisible when the test FAILS its condition; it only surfaces on a pass, which is when the conclusion gets used. |

**Two of the three open forecasts were not in the denominator until today, and both resolve
inside eleven weeks.**

**Not done here, deliberately:** nothing was scored (nothing has resolved), no probability was
re-weighted, and no ID was invented for #3/#5/#6. *Re-weighting #7 now on evidence about FTSE
mechanics is the post-hoc adjustment pre-registration exists to prevent — `assumptions.json`
